## Yantra Declarative UI DSL: the `app` macro, `{.component.}`, named props, events,
## reactive interpolation (L3/L4), reactive `if` and keyed `for` (L7), bound inputs.
##
## Everything here is a compile-time rewrite into plain calls on the SDK: node
## constructors, setters, `createEffect`, and the two control-flow procs in
## ui/control.nim. A prop, a text or a condition whose value is not a literal is
## wrapped in an effect, so it updates itself when the signals it reads change
## [S-PROP-3]. Event handlers run as one batch [S-RX-2].
import std/[macros, strutils]
import ../../protocol/schema

proc parseInterpolatedExpr*(s: string; info: NimNode): (NimNode, bool) =
  ## "Clicked {n} times" -> `"Clicked " & $(n) & " times"`, and whether it had any `{…}`.
  if not s.contains('{') or not s.contains('}'):
    return (newLit(s), false)
  var parts: seq[NimNode] = @[]
  var i = 0
  var cur = ""
  var hasInterp = false
  while i < s.len:
    if s[i] == '{':
      var closeIdx = s.find('}', i + 1)
      if closeIdx > i + 1:
        if cur.len > 0:
          parts.add(newLit(cur))
          cur = ""
        let exprStr = s[i + 1 .. closeIdx - 1].strip()
        let parsed = parseExpr(exprStr)
        parts.add(newCall(ident"$", parsed))
        hasInterp = true
        i = closeIdx + 1
        continue
    cur.add(s[i])
    inc i
  if cur.len > 0:
    parts.add(newLit(cur))
  if not hasInterp or parts.len == 0:
    return (newLit(s), false)
  var combined = parts[0]
  for idx in 1 ..< parts.len:
    combined = infix(combined, "&", parts[idx])
  result = (combined, true)

proc interpolateStr*(templateStr: string; vars: openArray[(string, string)]): string =
  ## Replaces all `{name}` occurrences in `templateStr` using `vars`.
  result = templateStr
  for (k, v) in vars:
    result = result.replace("{" & k & "}", v)

# --- helpers --------------------------------------------------------------------

proc isLiteral(n: NimNode): bool =
  case n.kind
  of nnkCharLit .. nnkFloat128Lit, nnkStrLit .. nnkTripleStrLit: true
  of nnkIdent, nnkSym: ($n) in ["true", "false", "nil"] or ($n).startsWith("al") and ($n).len > 2 and ($n)[2].isUpperAscii
  of nnkPrefix: n.len == 2 and isLiteral(n[1])
  else: false

proc lambda(body: NimNode; params: varargs[NimNode]): NimNode =
  var ps = @[newEmptyNode()]
  for p in params: ps.add(p)
  newProc(params = ps, body = body, procType = nnkLambda)

proc effect(stmt: NimNode): NimNode =
  newTree(nnkDiscardStmt, newCall(ident"createEffect", lambda(newStmtList(stmt))))

proc reactiveOrOnce(stmt: NimNode; value: NimNode): NimNode =
  if isLiteral(value): stmt else: effect(stmt)

const eventNames = [("onclick", "evClick"), ("onchange", "evChange"), ("oninput", "evInput"),
                    ("onsubmit", "evSubmit"), ("onfocus", "evFocus"), ("onblur", "evBlur"),
                    ("ondismiss", "evDismiss"), ("oncontextmenu", "evContextMenu"), ("onkey", "evKey"),
                    ("ondragstart", "evDragStart"), ("ondragover", "evDragOver"), ("ondrop", "evDrop"),
                    ("onframe", "evFrame"), ("onpointerdown", "evPointerDown"),
                    ("onpointermove", "evPointerMove"), ("onpointerup", "evPointerUp")]


proc eventOf(name: string): string =
  for (k, v) in eventNames:
    if k == name.toLowerAscii(): return v
  ""

proc headName(n: NimNode): string =
  if n.kind in {nnkCall, nnkCommand} and n.len > 0 and n[0].kind in {nnkIdent, nnkSym}: $n[0] else: ""

# --- props ------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Units (sdk/ui/units.nim): each of these props checks the unit a value carries.
const lengthProps = ["width", "height", "minwidth", "maxwidth", "minheight", "maxheight", "pad", "padding",
                     "padx", "pady", "padtop", "padright", "padbottom", "padleft", "margin", "marginx",
                     "marginy", "margintop", "marginright", "marginbottom", "marginleft", "gap", "spacing",
                     "radius", "border", "top", "left", "right", "bottom", "x", "y", "size", "fontsize",
                     "blur", "backdropblur"]
const durationProps = ["transition"]
const angleProps = ["rotate"]
# Theme words (docs/spec/style.md §4): in a colour prop, a bare `surface` means `theme().surface`.
const themeColors = ["bg", "sidebar", "surface", "surface2", "surface3", "line", "text", "muted", "faint",
                     "accent", "accentText", "accentSoft", "danger", "success"]
const colorProps = ["fill", "color", "textcolor", "bordercolor", "shadow"]

proc themeWords*(n: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## Rewrites bare theme colour names anywhere in a value — inside `if`, `rgba(…)`,
  ## `linear(…)`, `shadow(color = …)` — to reads of the current theme, so they re-style
  ## on a theme switch. Callee names, field names (`x.accent`) and argument names stay.
  case n.kind
  of nnkIdent:
    for w in themeColors:
      if eqIdent(n, w): return newDotExpr(newCall(ident"theme"), ident(w))
    n
  of nnkDotExpr:
    newTree(nnkDotExpr, themeWords(n[0]), n[1])
  of nnkCall, nnkCommand, nnkCallStrLit:
    var r = newTree(n.kind, n[0])
    for i in 1 ..< n.len: r.add themeWords(n[i])
    r
  of nnkExprEqExpr, nnkExprColonExpr:
    newTree(n.kind, n[0], themeWords(n[1]))
  of nnkLiterals, nnkSym, nnkOpenSymChoice, nnkClosedSymChoice:
    n
  else:
    var r = copyNimNode(n)
    for c in n: r.add themeWords(c)
    r

proc withUnits(norm: string; v: NimNode): NimNode =
  if norm in lengthProps: newCall(ident"lengthOf", v)
  elif norm in durationProps: newCall(ident"durationOf", v)
  elif norm in angleProps: newCall(ident"angleOf", v)
  else: v

proc setCall(nodeSym: NimNode; prop: string; v: NimNode): NimNode =
  newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"set"), ident(prop), v)

proc wireValue(propType: string; v: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## A prop value converted to its wire type.
  case propType
  of "int32": newCall(ident"wireI32", newCall(ident"int", v))
  of "float32": newCall(ident"float32", v)
  of "bool": newCall(ident"bool", v)
  else: newCall(ident"$", v)

proc buildPropSetter(nodeSym: NimNode; nodeKindName: string; arg: NimNode;
                     reactive = true; checkKind = true): NimNode =
  ## One `name = value` prop at a call site (or in a `style(...)` preset, where it
  ## is applied once, not wrapped in an effect, and is not tied to a node kind).
  let propName = $arg[0]
  var propVal = arg[1]
  let norm = propName.toLowerAscii()
  # words that only mean something in one prop: `justify = between`, `cursor = hand`
  if propVal.kind == nnkIdent:
    let w = ($propVal).toLowerAscii()
    let words = case norm
      of "alignitems": @[("first", 0), ("start", 0), ("center", 1), ("last", 2), ("stretch", 3)]
      of "justify": @[("first", 0), ("start", 0), ("center", 1), ("last", 2), ("between", 3), ("around", 4), ("evenly", 5)]
      of "cursor": @[("arrow", 0), ("hand", 1), ("ibeam", 2), ("text", 2)]
      of "align": @[("left", 0), ("center", 1), ("right", 2)]
      of "fit": @[("contain", 0), ("cover", 1), ("fill", 2)]
      of "blend": @[("normal", 3), ("plus", 12), ("screen", 14), ("overlay", 15), ("darken", 16), ("lighten", 17),
                    ("colordodge", 18), ("colorburn", 19), ("hardlight", 20), ("softlight", 21), ("difference", 22),
                    ("exclusion", 23), ("multiply", 24), ("hue", 25), ("saturation", 26), ("color", 27),
                    ("luminosity", 28)]
      else: @[]
    for (word, v) in words:
      if w == word: propVal = newLit(v)
    if norm == "ease":                   # `ease = outBack`: a curve from protocol/schema.nim `Easings`
      for i, e in Easings:
        if w == e.toLowerAscii or w == "ease" & e.toLowerAscii: propVal = newLit(i)
    if norm == "font" and w in ["mono", "font"]: propVal = newDotExpr(newCall(ident"theme"), ident(w))
  if norm == "fit" and propVal.kind in {nnkStrLit, nnkTripleStrLit}:
    let w = propVal.strVal.toLowerAscii
    propVal = newLit(if w == "cover": 1 elif w == "fill": 2 else: 0)
  if norm in colorProps: propVal = themeWords(propVal)
  if not isLiteral(propVal):                # a plain number needs no unit check
    propVal = withUnits(norm, propVal)
  var s: NimNode
  case norm
  of "padx":
    s = newStmtList(setCall(nodeSym, "pPadLeft", wireValue("int32", propVal)),
                    setCall(nodeSym, "pPadRight", wireValue("int32", propVal)))
  of "pady":
    s = newStmtList(setCall(nodeSym, "pPadTop", wireValue("int32", propVal)),
                    setCall(nodeSym, "pPadBottom", wireValue("int32", propVal)))
  of "margin", "marginx", "marginy":
    s = newStmtList()
    let sides = if norm == "marginx": @["Left", "Right"] elif norm == "marginy": @["Top", "Bottom"]
                else: @["Top", "Right", "Bottom", "Left"]
    for side in sides: s.add setCall(nodeSym, "pMargin" & side, wireValue("float32", propVal))
  of "fill":
    s = newCall(ident"setFill", nodeSym, propVal)            # a colour or a gradient
  of "shadow":
    s = newCall(ident"setShadow", nodeSym, propVal)
  of "use":
    s = newCall(ident"applyStyle", nodeSym, propVal)         # a style preset
  of "enter":
    s = newCall(ident"setEnter", nodeSym, propVal)           # played when the node appears
  of "exit":
    s = newCall(ident"setExit", nodeSym, propVal)            # played after the app removes it
  of "reveal":
    s = newCall(ident"setReveal", nodeSym, propVal)          # follows scrolling into view
  of "animate":
    s = newCall(ident"setLoop", nodeSym, propVal)            # a looping animation
  else:
    if findPropMeta(propName) < 0 or (checkKind and not isPropAllowedOnNode(propName, nodeKindName)):
      let sug = suggestPropName(propName, if checkKind: nodeKindName else: "*")
      let hint = if sug.len > 0: " — did you mean '" & sug & "'?" else: ""
      error("Y001 unknown prop '" & propName & "' on " & nodeKindName.toLowerAscii() & hint, arg)
    case norm
    of "gap", "spacing":
      s = newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"spacing"), newCall(ident"int32", propVal))
    of "pad", "padding":
      s = newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"padding"), newCall(ident"int32", propVal))
    of "size", "fontsize":
      s = newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"fontSize"), newCall(ident"int32", propVal))
    of "width":
      s = newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"width"), newCall(ident"int32", propVal))
    of "height":
      s = newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"height"), newCall(ident"int32", propVal))
    else:
      let meta = PropTable[findPropMeta(propName)]
      s = setCall(nodeSym, "p" & meta.wireProp, wireValue(meta.propType, propVal))
  if reactive: reactiveOrOnce(s, propVal) else: s

const stateBlocks = [("hover", "Hover"), ("pressed", "Press"), ("focused", "Focus")]

proc stateBlock(nodeSym: NimNode; prefix: string; blk: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## `hover:` / `pressed:` / `focused:` — props that apply while the state holds
  ## [S-STY-2]. The engine switches them; the app does no work.
  result = newStmtList()
  for st in blk:
    if st.kind != nnkAsgn:
      error("a state block holds assignments like `fill = …`", st)
    let name = ($st[0]).toLowerAscii()
    let (suffix, typ) = case name
      of "fill": ("Fill", "int32")
      of "color": ("Color", "int32")
      of "bordercolor": ("Border", "int32")
      of "opacity": ("Opacity", "float32")
      of "scale": ("Scale", "float32")
      of "y": ("Y", "float32")
      else: ("", "")
    if suffix.len == 0:
      error("Y013 '" & name & "' can't change with state — use fill, color, borderColor, opacity, scale or y", st)
    var v = st[1]
    if name in ["fill", "color", "bordercolor"]: v = themeWords(v)
    elif name == "y": v = newCall(ident"lengthOf", v)
    result.add reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "p" & prefix & suffix, wireValue(typ, v)), v)

# --- statements ---------------------------------------------------------------------

proc transformUiStmt(stmt: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode; assignTo: NimNode = nil): NimNode
proc transformUiBlock*(blk: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode): NimNode

proc eventHandler(nodeSym: NimNode; ev: string; body: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## node.on(evX, proc() = batch(proc() = body))
  let batched = newCall(ident"batch", lambda(body))
  newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"on"), ident(ev), lambda(newStmtList(batched)))

proc childBlock(nodeSym: NimNode; blk: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## Children of a node: events attach to the node itself, the rest are UI statements.
  transformUiBlock(blk, nodeSym)

proc textLike(ctor: string; nodeSym, parentSym, mainArg: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## A text/button: literal text is set once, interpolated or computed text updates itself.
  result = newStmtList()
  if mainArg.kind in {nnkStrLit, nnkTripleStrLit}:
    let (exprAst, isReactive) = parseInterpolatedExpr(mainArg.strVal, mainArg)
    if isReactive:
      result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident(ctor), parentSym, newLit(""))))
      result.add(effect(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"setText"), exprAst)))
    else:
      result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident(ctor), parentSym, mainArg)))
  else:
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident(ctor), parentSym, newLit(""))))
    result.add(effect(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"setText"), newCall(ident"$", mainArg))))

proc takeHandle(stmt: NimNode): string =
  ## `text @headline "Hi"` / `panel(@card, …)`: pulls out the `@name` handle (roadmap S3)
  ## and leaves the statement as it would be without it.
  if stmt.len < 2: return ""
  let a = stmt[1]
  if a.kind == nnkCall and a[0].kind == nnkPrefix and $a[0][0] == "@":
    # `panel @card(fill = …)` parses as a call of `@card`: the handle, then its arguments
    result = $a[0][1]
    stmt.del(1)
    for i in countdown(a.len - 1, 1): stmt.insert(1, a[i])
  elif a.kind == nnkPrefix and $a[0] == "@":
    result = $a[1]
    stmt.del(1)
  elif a.kind == nnkCommand and a.len == 2 and a[0].kind == nnkPrefix and $a[0][0] == "@":
    result = $a[0][1]
    stmt[1] = a[1]

proc nodeStmt(stmt: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode; assignTo: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## A node constructor: column/row/panel/group, text, button, input, checkbox, spacer.
  let handle = takeHandle(stmt)
  let name = ($stmt[0]).toLowerAscii()
  let nodeSym = genSym(nskLet, name & "Node")
  result = newStmtList()
  var firstArg = 1
  var kindCap = ""
  case name
  of "column", "row", "panel", "group":
    kindCap = if name == "column": "Column" elif name == "row": "Row" elif name == "panel": "Panel" else: "Group"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident(name), parentSym)))
  of "text", "button":
    kindCap = if name == "text": "Text" else: "Button"
    if stmt.len < 2: error(name & " needs its text as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(textLike(name, nodeSym, parentSym, stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  of "modal", "drawer", "popover", "menu", "contextmenu":
    # overlays (OV1): a layer above the window, not in the page's layout. A modal is centred
    # over a dimmed window, a drawer docks to an edge, a popover or menu opens by the node it
    # is inside, a context menu at a right click on that node. `open = sig` shows it (absent:
    # open while it exists); Escape or a click outside fires `onDismiss` (or just closes it)
    kindCap = case name
              of "modal": "Modal"
              of "drawer": "Drawer"
              of "popover": "Popover"
              of "menu": "Menu"
              else: "ContextMenu"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident("nk" & kindCap), parentSym)))
  of "tooltip":
    # `tooltip "Copy link"` inside a node: shown after the pointer rests on that node
    kindCap = "Tooltip"
    if stmt.len < 2: error("tooltip needs its text as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkTooltip", parentSym)))
    result.add(reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "pText", newCall(ident"$", stmt[1])), stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  of "model":
    # `model "fox.glb", width = 400.px, height = 300.px` — a glTF 2.0 model from the app's
    # folder, lit and framed, its first animation playing (Filament)
    kindCap = "Model"
    if stmt.len < 2: error("model needs its file name as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkModel", parentSym)))
    result.add(reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "pSource", newCall(ident"$", stmt[1])), stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  of "scene3d", "camera", "light", "mesh", "box", "sphere", "plane", "cylinder":
    # native 3D (S3D): `scene3d(sky = 0x87CEEB, fog = 0.02):` is a view into a 3D world; inside
    # it `box`/`sphere`/`plane`/`cylinder`/`mesh(geometry = buf)` are objects (posX.., rotY..,
    # sizeX.., fill, metal, rough, glow, instances = buf), `light` and `camera` light and see
    # it, and `model "fox.glb"` is an object too. Filament draws it; the objects are not laid out.
    kindCap = case name
              of "scene3d": "Scene3D"
              of "camera": "Camera"
              of "light": "Light"
              else: "Mesh"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident("nk" & kindCap), parentSym)))
    let shapeOf = ["box", "sphere", "plane", "cylinder"].find(name)
    if shapeOf > 0: result.add(setCall(nodeSym, "pShape", wireValue("int32", newLit(shapeOf))))
  of "canvas":
    # `canvas(draw = d.cmds, width = 400.px, height = 300.px)`: 2D drawing (paint.nim's Draw:
    # paths, shapes, text, images, layers, raw pixels, blend modes); pointer events on it
    kindCap = "Canvas"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkCanvas", parentSym)))
  of "particles":
    # `particles(posY = 2, rate = 200, lifetime = 1.5, speed = 4, spread = 30, fill = 0xFFFFB000,
    # colorEnd = 0x00FF2000, gravity = 9.8)`: sprites the engine emits and moves (PT1)
    kindCap = "Particles"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkParticles", parentSym)))
  of "synth":
    # `synth(freq = pitch, wave = 3, filter = 1, cutoff = 800, playing = held)`: a sound the
    # engine makes (AU1) — wave or noise, filtered, enveloped, echoed, panned; not laid out
    kindCap = "Synth"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkSynth", parentSym)))
  of "rich":
    # `rich "Some **bold** and [a link](https://x.com)"`: one Text node, runs styled (R2)
    kindCap = "Text"
    if stmt.len < 2: error("rich needs its markup as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkText", parentSym)))
    result.add(reactiveOrOnce(newCall(ident"setRich", nodeSym, stmt[1]), stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  of "input", "textarea":
    # `input draft` / `textArea notes`: one line or many, bound to a Signal[string]
    kindCap = if name == "input": "Input" else: "TextArea"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, if name == "input": newCall(ident"input", parentSym)
                                   else: newCall(ident"make", ident"nkTextArea", parentSym)))
    if stmt.len >= 2 and stmt[1].kind notin {nnkExprEqExpr, nnkStmtList}:
      # `input draft`: two-way binding to a Signal[string] [S-CMP-3]
      let sig = stmt[1]
      result.add(effect(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"set"), ident"pValue",
                                newCall(ident"get", sig))))
      result.add(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"on"), ident"evInput",
        lambda(newStmtList(newCall(ident"set", sig,
          newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"getStr"), ident"pValue"))))))
      firstArg = 2
  of "checkbox":
    kindCap = "Checkbox"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkCheckbox", parentSym)))
  of "stack":
    # children overlap; each is placed by its own top/left/right/bottom
    kindCap = "Stack"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkStack", parentSym)))
  of "path":
    kindCap = "Path"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkPath", parentSym)))
  of "icon":
    # `icon "search", size = 18, color = …` — a path from the standard icon set
    kindCap = "Path"
    if stmt.len < 2: error("icon needs its name as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkPath", parentSym)))
    result.add(setCall(nodeSym, "pStrokeWidth", newCall(ident"float32", newLit(1.8))))
    result.add(reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "pPathData", newCall(ident"iconPath", stmt[1])), stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  of "video":
    # `video "clip.mp4", fit = cover, loop = true` — a file from the app's own folder
    kindCap = "Video"
    if stmt.len < 2: error("video needs its file name as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkVideo", parentSym)))
    result.add(reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "pSource", newCall(ident"$", stmt[1])), stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  of "page", "slide":
    # a page of a document, or a 1280x720 slide of a deck: one PDF page each (D1)
    kindCap = "Page"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkPage", parentSym)))
    if name == "slide":
      result.add(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"width"), newLit(1280'i32)))
      result.add(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"height"), newLit(720'i32)))
  of "grid":
    # `grid "1fr 2fr 200", spacing = 12:` — columns of fixed px, shares (fr) or `auto`;
    # children fill them in order, row after row (`span = 2` takes two columns)
    kindCap = "Grid"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkGrid", parentSym)))
    if stmt.len >= 2 and stmt[1].kind notin {nnkExprEqExpr, nnkStmtList}:
      result.add(reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "pGridCols", newCall(ident"$", stmt[1])), stmt[1]))
      firstArg = 2
  of "appview":
    # another app, sandboxed, drawn in this box: `appView(source = "todo", grow = 1)`
    kindCap = "AppView"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkAppView", parentSym)))
  of "spacer":
    kindCap = "Spacer"
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkSpacer", parentSym)))
    result.add(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"set"), ident"pFlexGrow", newCall(ident"float32", newLit(1))))
  of "image":
    kindCap = "Image"
    if stmt.len < 2: error("image needs its source path as the first argument", stmt)
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident"make", ident"nkImage", parentSym)))
    result.add(reactiveOrOnce(setCall(nodeSym, "pSource", newCall(ident"$", stmt[1])), stmt[1]))
    firstArg = 2
  else:
    error("unknown node kind " & name, stmt)
  if assignTo != nil:
    result.add(newAssignment(assignTo, nodeSym))
  if handle.len > 0:                    # a stable name for code, tests, tools and AI edits
    result.add newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"set"), ident"pName", newLit(handle))
  # where in the source this node comes from (dev builds only): hot reload keeps focus,
  # scroll and running child apps by it, and inspectors jump from a pixel to its code
  let li = stmt.lineInfoObj
  let site = li.filename.replace('\\', '/').rsplit('/', 1)[^1] & ":" & $li.line & ":" & $li.column
  result.add newTree(nnkWhenStmt, newTree(nnkElifBranch, prefix(newCall(ident"defined", ident"release"), "not"),
    newStmtList(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"set"), ident"pSite", newLit(site)))))
  for i in firstArg ..< stmt.len:
    let arg = stmt[i]
    if arg.kind == nnkExprEqExpr:
      result.add(buildPropSetter(nodeSym, kindCap, arg))
    elif arg.kind == nnkStmtList:
      result.add(childBlock(nodeSym, arg))
    else:
      error("unexpected positional argument to " & name & " (Y010 positional after main)", arg)

const nodeKinds = ["column", "row", "panel", "group", "text", "button", "input", "textarea", "checkbox", "spacer", "appview",
                   "stack", "path", "icon", "video", "page", "slide", "image", "grid", "rich", "model",
                   "modal", "drawer", "popover", "menu", "contextmenu", "tooltip",
                   "scene3d", "camera", "light", "mesh", "box", "sphere", "plane", "cylinder", "synth", "canvas", "particles"]

proc forStmt(stmt: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode; emptyBlk: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## `for x in xs.keyed(key): body` / `for x in xs: body` [S-CTL-2] -> keyedList(...)
  if stmt.len != 3 or stmt[0].kind != nnkIdent:
    error("for in a UI block takes one loop variable: `for x in items.keyed(x.id):`", stmt)
  let loopVar = stmt[0]
  var src = stmt[1]
  var keyExpr = newCall(ident"$", loopVar)          # default key: the item itself
  if src.kind == nnkCall and src[0].kind == nnkDotExpr and $src[0][1] == "keyed" and src.len == 2:
    keyExpr = newCall(ident"$", src[1])
    src = src[0][0]
  let listVal = newCall(ident"listValue", src)
  let itemT = newCall(ident"typeof", newTree(nnkBracketExpr, listVal, newLit(0)))
  let listT = newCall(ident"typeof", listVal)
  let parentParam = ident"yParent"
  let itemsProc = newProc(params = [listT], body = newStmtList(listVal), procType = nnkLambda)
  let keyProc = newProc(params = [ident"string", newIdentDefs(loopVar, itemT)],
                        body = newStmtList(keyExpr), procType = nnkLambda)
  let buildProc = newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(parentParam, ident"Node"), newIdentDefs(loopVar, itemT)],
                          body = transformUiBlock(stmt[2], parentParam), procType = nnkLambda)
  var call = newCall(ident"keyedList", parentSym, itemsProc, keyProc, buildProc)
  if emptyBlk != nil:
    call.add(newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(parentParam, ident"Node")],
                     body = transformUiBlock(emptyBlk, parentParam), procType = nnkLambda))
  newStmtList(call)

proc ifStmt(stmt: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## if/elif/else -> showWhen(parent, branchIndex, builds) [S-CTL-1]
  let parentParam = ident"yParent"
  var chooser = newTree(nnkIfExpr)
  var builds = newTree(nnkBracket)
  var hasElse = false
  for i, br in stmt:
    let buildProc = newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(parentParam, ident"Node")],
                            body = transformUiBlock(br[^1], parentParam), procType = nnkLambda)
    buildProc.addPragma(ident"closure")   # one element type for the whole seq
    builds.add(buildProc)
    if br.kind == nnkElifBranch:
      chooser.add(newTree(nnkElifExpr, br[0], newLit(i)))
    else:
      chooser.add(newTree(nnkElseExpr, newLit(i)))
      hasElse = true
  if not hasElse: chooser.add(newTree(nnkElseExpr, newLit(-1)))
  let branchProc = newProc(params = [ident"int"], body = newStmtList(chooser), procType = nnkLambda)
  newStmtList(newCall(ident"showWhen", parentSym, branchProc, prefix(builds, "@")))

proc caseStmt(stmt: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## case -> showWhen(parent, branchIndex, builds) [S-CTL-1]
  let parentParam = ident"yParent"
  var chooser = newTree(nnkCaseStmt, stmt[0])
  var builds = newTree(nnkBracket)
  var hasElse = false
  var branchIdx = 0
  for i in 1 ..< stmt.len:
    let br = stmt[i]
    if br.kind == nnkOfBranch:
      let buildProc = newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(parentParam, ident"Node")],
                              body = transformUiBlock(br[^1], parentParam), procType = nnkLambda)
      buildProc.addPragma(ident"closure")
      builds.add(buildProc)
      var ofBr = newTree(nnkOfBranch)
      for j in 0 ..< br.len - 1: ofBr.add(br[j])
      ofBr.add(newLit(branchIdx))
      chooser.add(ofBr)
      inc branchIdx
    elif br.kind == nnkElse:
      let buildProc = newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(parentParam, ident"Node")],
                              body = transformUiBlock(br[0], parentParam), procType = nnkLambda)
      buildProc.addPragma(ident"closure")
      builds.add(buildProc)
      chooser.add(newTree(nnkElse, newLit(branchIdx)))
      hasElse = true
      inc branchIdx
  if not hasElse: chooser.add(newTree(nnkElse, newLit(-1)))
  let branchProc = newProc(params = [ident"int"], body = newStmtList(chooser), procType = nnkLambda)
  newStmtList(newCall(ident"showWhen", parentSym, branchProc, prefix(builds, "@")))

proc transformUiStmt(stmt: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode; assignTo: NimNode = nil): NimNode =
  if stmt.kind == nnkStmtList:           # a block inside a block (macros make these)
    return transformUiBlock(stmt, parentSym)
  if stmt.kind == nnkAsgn and stmt[1].kind in {nnkCall, nnkCommand}:
    # `x = column(...)` keeps the node in x; `x = anyOtherCall(...)` is plain Nim, kept as is
    let head = headName(stmt[1])
    if head.len > 0 and (head.toLowerAscii() in nodeKinds or head[0].isUpperAscii()):
      return transformUiStmt(stmt[1], parentSym, assignTo = stmt[0])
    return stmt
  if stmt.kind == nnkIfStmt:
    return ifStmt(stmt, parentSym)
  if stmt.kind == nnkCaseStmt:
    return caseStmt(stmt, parentSym)
  if stmt.kind in {nnkCall, nnkCommand} and stmt.len > 0 and stmt[0].kind in {nnkIdent, nnkSym}:
    let name = $stmt[0]
    if name[0].isUpperAscii():
      # A component: `Card("x", 3)` -> `Card(parent, "x", 3)` [S-CMP-1]
      var call = newCall(stmt[0], parentSym)
      for i in 1 ..< stmt.len:
        if i == stmt.len - 1 and stmt[i].kind == nnkStmtList:
          # the caller's block becomes the component's children (its `slot()`)
          let kids = newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(ident"yParent", ident"Node")],
                             body = transformUiBlock(stmt[i], ident"yParent"), procType = nnkLambda)
          kids.addPragma(ident"closure")
          call.add(newTree(nnkExprEqExpr, ident"yChildren", kids))
        else:
          call.add(stmt[i])
      return call
    let low = name.toLowerAscii()
    if low in nodeKinds:
      return nodeStmt(stmt, parentSym, assignTo)
    let ev = eventOf(low)
    if ev.len > 0 and stmt.len >= 2 and stmt[^1].kind == nnkStmtList:
      return eventHandler(parentSym, ev, stmt[^1])
    for (st, prefix) in stateBlocks:
      if low == st and stmt.len == 2 and stmt[1].kind == nnkStmtList:
        return stateBlock(parentSym, prefix, stmt[1])
    if low == "sized" and stmt.len >= 3 and stmt[^1].kind == nnkStmtList:
      # `sized w:` / `sized w, h:` — the container's laid-out size as signals (px), so
      # an `if w < 640:` inside rebuilds when it crosses: container queries [S-LAY-2]
      result = newStmtList()
      let wSym = if stmt[1].kind in {nnkIdent, nnkSym}: stmt[1] else: genSym(nskLet, "w")
      let hSym = if stmt.len >= 4 and stmt[2].kind in {nnkIdent, nnkSym}: stmt[2] else: genSym(nskLet, "h")
      result.add newLetStmt(wSym, newCall(ident"newState", newLit(0'f32)))
      result.add newLetStmt(hSym, newCall(ident"newState", newLit(0'f32)))
      result.add newCall(ident"trackSize", parentSym, wSym, hSym)
      result.add transformUiBlock(stmt[^1], parentSym)
      return result
    if low == "scrolled" and stmt.len == 3 and stmt[^1].kind == nnkStmtList:
      # `scrolled y:` — the container's scroll offset as a signal (px)
      result = newStmtList()
      let ySym = if stmt[1].kind in {nnkIdent, nnkSym}: stmt[1] else: genSym(nskLet, "y")
      result.add newLetStmt(ySym, newCall(ident"newState", newLit(0'f32)))
      result.add newCall(ident"trackScroll", parentSym, ySym)
      result.add transformUiBlock(stmt[^1], parentSym)
      return result
    if low == "lazy" and stmt.len == 4 and stmt[1].kind == nnkInfix and $stmt[1][0] == "in" and
       stmt[^1].kind == nnkStmtList:
      # `lazy m in msgs.get, 44:` — a scrolling list that builds only the rows that show
      # (rows `44` px tall), with spacers for the rest: 100 000 items cost what 20 do
      let loopVar = stmt[1][1]
      var src = stmt[1][2]
      var keyCall: NimNode = nil
      if src.kind == nnkCall and src[0].kind == nnkDotExpr and $src[0][1] == "keyed" and src.len == 2:
        keyCall = src[1]
        src = src[0][0]
      let rh = newCall(ident"float32", stmt[2])
      let wS = genSym(nskLet, "lzW")
      let hS = genSym(nskLet, "lzH")
      let yS = genSym(nskLet, "lzY")
      let first = newCall(ident"lazyFirst", newCall(ident"get", yS), rh)
      let last = newCall(ident"lazyLast", newCall(ident"get", yS), newCall(ident"get", hS), rh,
                         newCall(ident"len", src))
      var slice = newTree(nnkBracketExpr, src, infix(first, "..<", last))
      if keyCall != nil: slice = newCall(newDotExpr(slice, ident"keyed"), keyCall)
      let rowBody = stmt[^1]              # quote takes plain names only
      let body = quote do:
        column(grow = 1, scroll = true, minHeight = 0):
          sized `wS`, `hS`:
            scrolled `yS`:
              panel(height = float32(`first`) * `rh`)
              column:
                for `loopVar` in `slice`:
                  `rowBody`
              panel(height = float32(len(`src`) - `last`) * `rh`)
      return transformUiBlock(body, parentSym)
    if low == "slot" and stmt.len == 1:
      # the caller's block, placed here [S-CMP-4]
      return newIfStmt((infix(ident"yChildren", "!=", newNilLit()), newCall(ident"yChildren", parentSym)))
  result = stmt

proc transformUiBlock*(blk: NimNode; parentSym: NimNode): NimNode =
  result = newStmtList()
  if blk.kind != nnkStmtList:
    result.add(transformUiStmt(blk, parentSym))
    return
  var i = 0
  while i < blk.len:
    let s = blk[i]
    if s.kind == nnkForStmt:
      var emptyBlk: NimNode = nil
      if i + 1 < blk.len and headName(blk[i + 1]).toLowerAscii() == "empty" and blk[i + 1][^1].kind == nnkStmtList:
        emptyBlk = blk[i + 1][^1]
        inc i
      result.add(forStmt(s, parentSym, emptyBlk))
    else:
      result.add(transformUiStmt(s, parentSym))
    inc i

# --- app and component ------------------------------------------------------------

proc expandAppAst(title: NimNode; body: NimNode; props: seq[NimNode] = @[]): NimNode =
  let winSym = genSym(nskLet, "winNode")
  let inner = newStmtList()
  var winProps: seq[NimNode] = @[]
  for p in props:
    if p.kind == nnkExprEqExpr and ($p[0]).toLowerAscii() == "needs":
      let val = p[1]
      if val.kind == nnkBracket:
        var call = newCall(ident"needs")
        for elem in val: call.add elem
        inner.add call
      else:
        inner.add newCall(ident"needs", val)
    else:
      winProps.add p
  inner.add(newLetStmt(winSym, newCall(ident"window", title)))
  for p in winProps: inner.add(buildPropSetter(winSym, "Window", p))
  inner.add(transformUiBlock(body, winSym))
  result = newCall(ident"yantraRun", inner)

macro app*(title: string; args: varargs[untyped]): untyped =
  ## `app "Title", width = 480, height = 720:` then the UI block.
  var props: seq[NimNode] = @[]
  var body: NimNode = newStmtList()
  for a in args:
    if a.kind == nnkExprEqExpr: props.add(a)
    elif a.kind == nnkStmtList: body = a
  result = expandAppAst(title, body, props)

macro component*(p: untyped): untyped =
  ## `proc Card(title: string) {.component.} = <UI block>`: an ordinary proc whose
  ## body is a UI block. It gets a hidden first parameter, the parent node, which
  ## the caller's UI block supplies [S-CMP-1].
  if p.kind != nnkProcDef: error("{.component.} goes on a proc", p)
  result = p.copyNimTree()
  let params = result.params
  var np = newTree(nnkFormalParams, params[0], newIdentDefs(ident"yParent", ident"Node"))
  for i in 1 ..< params.len: np.add(params[i])
  # the caller's block, if any, for `slot()` [S-CMP-4]
  np.add(newIdentDefs(ident"yChildren",
    newTree(nnkProcTy, newTree(nnkFormalParams, newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(ident"p", ident"Node")),
            newTree(nnkPragma, ident"closure")), newNilLit()))
  result.params = np
  result.body = transformUiBlock(p.body, ident"yParent")

macro expandAppDsl*(body: untyped): string =
  var target = body
  if target.kind == nnkStmtList and target.len == 1:
    target = target[0]
  if target.kind in {nnkCall, nnkCommand} and ($target[0]).toLowerAscii() == "app":
    let ast = expandAppAst(target[1], target[^1])
    result = newLit(ast.repr)
  else:
    let ast = expandAppAst(newLit("App"), target)
    result = newLit(ast.repr)


macro style*(args: varargs[untyped]): untyped =
  ## `style(fill = …, radius = 12, pad = 16)`: a reusable set of props, applied with
  ## `use = …` [S-STY-3]. Call-site props written after `use` win.
  let n = ident"yStyled"
  var procs = newTree(nnkBracket)
  for a in args:
    if a.kind != nnkExprEqExpr: error("style takes named props: style(fill = …, radius = …)", a)
    let lam = newProc(params = [newEmptyNode(), newIdentDefs(n, ident"Node")],
                      body = newStmtList(buildPropSetter(n, "*", a, reactive = false, checkKind = false)),
                      procType = nnkLambda)
    lam.addPragma(ident"closure")
    procs.add lam
  if procs.len == 0:
    result = newCall(ident"Style")
  else:
    result = newTree(nnkObjConstr, ident"Style", newColonExpr(ident"apply", prefix(procs, "@")))


================================================================
FILE: sdk/ui/paint.nim
================================================================
## 2D drawing (P7, CV2): a `Draw` list for a canvas (`canvas(draw = d.cmds)`), and layers:
## bitmaps an app keeps and draws into over time — a painting's strokes, a simulation's frames.
##
##   let paper = newLayer(800, 600)
##   paper.draw(Draw().moveTo(10, 10).lineTo(200, 80).stroke(0xFF000000'u32, 6))   # once: it stays
##   paper.filter(blur, 4)
##   canvas(draw = Draw().layer(paper, 0, 0).cmds, width = 800.px, height = 600.px)
import std/strutils

type
  Draw* = object
    cmds*: string                ## the command list (format: libs/nimskia skia_shim.cpp runCmds)
  Layer* = distinct int32
  Blend* = enum                  ## Skia's blend modes, in its order
    bClear, bSrc, bDst, bSrcOver, bDstOver, bSrcIn, bDstIn, bSrcOut, bDstOut, bSrcATop, bDstATop,
    bXor, bPlus, bModulate, bScreen, bOverlay, bDarken, bLighten, bColorDodge, bColorBurn,
    bHardLight, bSoftLight, bDifference, bExclusion, bMultiply, bHue, bSaturation, bColor, bLuminosity
  Filter* = enum
    blur, brightness, contrast, saturation, invert, greyscale, sepia, fade

const eraser* = bDstOut           ## draw with this blend to erase what is under the stroke

proc f(x: float32): string =
  ## Two decimals, without formatFloat (the sandbox's C library does not format floats).
  let v = int(abs(x) * 100 + 0.5)
  (if x < 0 and v > 0: "-" else: "") & $(v div 100) & "." & align($(v mod 100), 2, '0')
proc add(d: var Draw; s: string) = d.cmds.add s & ";"
proc moveTo*(d: Draw; x, y: float32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "M " & f(x) & " " & f(y))
proc lineTo*(d: Draw; x, y: float32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "L " & f(x) & " " & f(y))
proc quadTo*(d: Draw; cx, cy, x, y: float32): Draw =
  ## Corner arc, matching canvas quadraticCurveTo.
  result = d
  result.add "Q " & f(cx) & " " & f(cy) & " " & f(x) & " " & f(y)

proc curveTo*(d: Draw; cx1, cy1, cx2, cy2, x, y: float32): Draw =
  ## Cubic Bézier, matching canvas bezierCurveTo. Used by the ellipse shape.
  result = d
  result.add "C " & f(cx1) & " " & f(cy1) & " " & f(cx2) & " " & f(cy2) &
             " " & f(x) & " " & f(y)
proc arcTo*(d: Draw; cx, cy, radius, startDeg, sweepDeg: float32): Draw =
  ## Circular arc, matching canvas arc(). Angles in degrees, clockwise.
  result = d
  result.add "A " & f(cx) & " " & f(cy) & " " & f(radius) & " " &
             f(startDeg) & " " & f(sweepDeg)
proc close*(d: Draw): Draw = (result = d; result.add "Z")
proc svgPath*(d: Draw; path: string; x, y, scale: float32; argb: uint32;
              strokeWidth = 0'f32): Draw =
  ## SVG path data (any command set Skia parses: relative, H/V, smooth curves,
  ## arcs) at (x, y) scaled by `scale`. Filled unless strokeWidth > 0.
  result = d
  result.add "V " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(scale) & " " & f(strokeWidth) &
             " " & $argb & " " & path
proc fill*(d: Draw; argb: uint32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "F " & $argb)
  ## Fills the path so far.
proc stroke*(d: Draw; argb: uint32; width: float32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "S " & $argb & " " & f(width))
  ## Strokes the path so far (round joins and caps: a brush).
proc rect*(d: Draw; x, y, w, h: float32; argb: uint32; radius = 0'f32): Draw =
  result = d; result.add "R " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(w) & " " & f(h) & " " & f(radius) & " " & $argb
proc circle*(d: Draw; cx, cy, r: float32; argb: uint32): Draw =
  result = d; result.add "E " & f(cx) & " " & f(cy) & " " & f(r) & " " & f(r) & " " & $argb
proc ellipse*(d: Draw; cx, cy, rx, ry: float32; argb: uint32): Draw =
  ## An axis-aligned ellipse. The shim's `E` verb has always taken SEPARATE rx and ry
  ## (`skia_shim.cpp` case 'E' -> `drawOval(LTRB(cx-rx, cy-ry, cx+rx, cy+ry))`); only
  ## `circle` collapsed them by passing `r` twice, which made ellipses look unreachable
  ## and pushed apps into tracing polylines instead. rx == ry is a circle, so this is a
  ## strict superset of `circle` — nothing that used `circle` needs to change.
  result = d; result.add "E " & f(cx) & " " & f(cy) & " " & f(rx) & " " & f(ry) & " " & $argb
proc line*(d: Draw; x1, y1, x2, y2: float32; argb: uint32; width = 1'f32): Draw =
  result = d; result.add "G " & f(x1) & " " & f(y1) & " " & f(x2) & " " & f(y2) & " " & $argb & " " & f(width)
proc text*(d: Draw; x, y, size: float32; argb: uint32; s: string; weight = 400): Draw =
  result = d; result.add "T " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(size) & " " & $argb & " " & $weight & " " & s.replace(";", "\\;")
proc textFace*(d: Draw; x, y, size: float32; argb: uint32; family, s: string; weight = 400): Draw =
  ## Draws `s` in `family`, falling back to the engine default when it is "".
  ## DECISION_QUEUE D6. `text` above cannot name a typeface at all: it emits the `T`
  ## verb, the shim's `case 'T'` calls `yk_text_draw` which takes no family, and
  ## `yk_text_draw2` — which does — is reachable only from native nimskia, not from
  ## runCmds. So every direct-draw string was Segoe UI and an app could not change it.
  ##
  ## `family` and `s` are two separate `;`-terminated fields with **NO space between
  ## them**: `rest()` stops at the `;`, so a space here would become part of the name.
  ## An empty `family` takes the existing `!fam.empty()` fallback, so it renders
  ## identically to `text`.
  result = d; result.add "N " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(size) & " " & $argb & " " & $weight & " " &
    family.replace(";", "\\;") & ";" & s.replace(";", "\\;")
proc image*(d: Draw; x, y, w, h: float32; file: string): Draw =
  result = d; result.add "I " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(w) & " " & f(h) & " 0 " & file
proc layer*(d: Draw; l: Layer; x, y: float32; w = 0'f32; h = 0'f32): Draw =
  ## Shows layer `l` at (x, y), at its own size unless w, h are given.
  result = d; result.add "Y " & $int32(l) & " " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(w) & " " & f(h)
proc pixels*(d: Draw; buf: int32; pw, ph: int; x, y: float32; w = 0'f32; h = 0'f32): Draw =
  ## Raw RGBA pixels (pw x ph) from a buffer (newBuffer + setData), crisp when scaled up.
  result = d; result.add "X " & $buf & " " & $pw & " " & $ph & " " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(w) & " " & f(h)
proc blend*(d: Draw; mode: Blend): Draw = (result = d; result.add "b " & $ord(mode))
  ## How what follows combines with what is under it (multiply, screen, eraser…).
proc save*(d: Draw): Draw = (result = d; result.add "P")
proc restore*(d: Draw): Draw = (result = d; result.add "p")
proc translate*(d: Draw; x, y: float32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "t " & f(x) & " " & f(y))
proc scale*(d: Draw; sx: float32; sy = 0'f32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "s " & f(sx) & " " & f(sy))
proc rotate*(d: Draw; degrees: float32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "o " & f(degrees))
proc alpha*(d: Draw; a: float32): Draw = (result = d; result.add "O " & f(a))
proc clip*(d: Draw; x, y, w, h: float32): Draw =
  result = d; result.add "K " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(w) & " " & f(h)

var layerSeq: int32

when defined(yantra_headless) or (defined(wasm) and not defined(yantra_engine)):
  # No rasteriser linked (unit tests, plain `nim c`): every layer op is a no-op.
  proc yLayerNew(id, w, h: int32): int32 = 1
  proc yLayerDraw(id, p, n: int32) = discard
  proc yLayerFilter(id, kind: int32; amount: float32) = discard
  proc yLayerRead(id, x, y, w, h, p: int32): int32 = 0
  proc yLayerWrite(id, x, y, w, h, p: int32) = discard
  proc yLayerFree(id: int32) = discard
  proc yTextMeasure(sPtr, sLen, famPtr, famLen: int32; size: float32; weight: int32): float32 = 0
else:
  proc yLayerNew(id, w, h: int32): int32 {.importc: "y_layer_new", cdecl.}
  proc yLayerDraw(id, p, n: int32) {.importc: "y_layer_draw", cdecl.}
  proc yLayerFilter(id, kind: int32; amount: float32) {.importc: "y_layer_filter", cdecl.}
  proc yLayerRead(id, x, y, w, h, p: int32): int32 {.importc: "y_layer_read", cdecl.}
  proc yLayerWrite(id, x, y, w, h, p: int32) {.importc: "y_layer_write", cdecl.}
  proc yLayerFree(id: int32) {.importc: "y_layer_free", cdecl.}
  proc yTextMeasure(sPtr, sLen, famPtr, famLen: int32; size: float32; weight: int32): float32
      {.importc: "y_text_measure", cdecl.}

proc newLayer*(w, h: int): Layer =
  ## A transparent w x h bitmap.
  inc layerSeq
  discard yLayerNew(layerSeq, int32(w), int32(h))
  Layer(layerSeq)
proc draw*(l: Layer; d: Draw) =
  ## Draws into the layer once; it stays there (strokes accumulate).
  if d.cmds.len > 0: yLayerDraw(int32(l), cast[int32](d.cmds.cstring), int32(d.cmds.len))
proc clear*(l: Layer) = l.draw(Draw().blend(bClear).rect(0, 0, 1e6, 1e6, 0'u32))
proc filter*(l: Layer; kind: Filter; amount = 1'f32) = yLayerFilter(int32(l), int32(ord(kind)), amount)
  ## In place: blur (px), brightness (-1..1), contrast / saturation (1 same), invert, greyscale,
  ## sepia, fade (opacity 0..1).
proc read*(l: Layer; x, y, w, h: int): seq[byte] =
  ## w x h pixels as RGBA bytes (colour picker, saving, analysis).
  result = newSeq[byte](max(0, w * h * 4))
  if result.len > 0:
    let n = yLayerRead(int32(l), int32(x), int32(y), int32(w), int32(h), cast[int32](addr result[0]))
    if n != result.len: result.setLen(0)
proc write*(l: Layer; x, y, w, h: int; rgba: openArray[byte]) =
  ## w x h RGBA bytes into the layer at (x, y) (procedural images, simulations).
  if rgba.len >= w * h * 4 and w > 0 and h > 0:
    yLayerWrite(int32(l), int32(x), int32(y), int32(w), int32(h), cast[int32](unsafeAddr rgba[0]))
proc free*(l: Layer) = yLayerFree(int32(l))

proc textWidth*(s: string; size: float32; family = ""; weight = 400): float32 =
  ## How wide `s` is when drawn at `size` px in `family`, measured by the renderer with the
  ## real font. **Use this instead of guessing a per-character advance.** A flat
  ## `size * 0.6` is wrong for most fonts: Palatino's natural advance runs from 0.327em for
  ## '-' to 0.993em for 'W', a 0.667em spread, and dna's `MONO_ADV = 0.5498` was a hand-built
  ## table for one font that would be wrong for any other.
  ##
  ##   let w = textWidth("RESIGN", 13, "Palatino Linotype")   # right-align it exactly
  ##   for i, ch in "A HOT-SEAT GAME FOR TWO":                # letter-space it exactly
  ##     d.textFace(x + cx, y, size, col, "Palatino Linotype", ch)
  ##     cx += textWidth($ch, size, "Palatino Linotype") + track
  ##
  ## Pass the same `family` you draw with, or the measurement describes a different font.
  ## An empty `family` means the renderer's default, which is what `Draw.text` uses.
  ## Returns 0 for an empty string, or where no renderer is attached (headless tests).
  if s.len == 0: return 0
  yTextMeasure(cast[int32](s.cstring), int32(s.len), cast[int32](family.cstring),
               int32(family.len), size, int32(weight))

# --- additive Draw conveniences (ported from app hand-rolls; no existing API changed) ---
#
# Grep 2026-11-05: `textBaseline`/`textCentered` exist ONLY as private app
# helpers (typewriter/app.nim, no `*`); `strokeRect` exists nowhere as a proc
# (only a dna comment noting its absence); `strokeCircle` exists ONLY as a
# private gallery helper. SDK had zero hits, so these names are free here.
# App-local versions shadow the SDK on import (locals win) — no break; apps
# can delete their copies and use these (which use the real `textWidth`
# instead of per-font estimates).

proc textBaseline*(d: Draw; x, baseY, size: float32; argb: uint32;
                   s: string; weight = 400): Draw =
  ## Baseline-anchored text (default face): `baseY` sits on the alphabetic
  ## baseline. `Draw.text` takes the TOP of the line box, which is
  ## `baseY - 1.0833 * size` (measured Segoe ratios; see 06-text-and-fonts §6).
  d.text(x, baseY - 1.0833'f32 * size, size, argb, s, weight)

proc textFaceBaseline*(d: Draw; x, baseY, size: float32; argb: uint32;
                       family, s: string; weight = 400): Draw =
  ## Like `textBaseline` with an explicit typeface (mirrors `textFace`).
  d.textFace(x, baseY - 1.0833'f32 * size, size, argb, family, s, weight)

proc textCentered*(d: Draw; cx, baseY, size: float32; argb: uint32;
                   s: string; weight = 400): Draw =
  ## Horizontally centered + baseline-anchored (SVG `text-anchor="middle"` +
  ## `textBaseline="alphabetic"`). Width comes from the renderer's
  ## `textWidth` (exact for the face); headless it measures 0, so text starts
  ## at `cx` there. Chess `centred` is the top-box twin: add `1.0833 * size`.
  let w = textWidth(s, size, "", weight)
  d.text(cx - w * 0.5'f32, baseY - 1.0833'f32 * size, size, argb, s, weight)

proc textFaceCentered*(d: Draw; cx, baseY, size: float32; argb: uint32;
                       family, s: string; weight = 400): Draw =
  ## Like `textCentered` with an explicit typeface (pass the SAME family to
  ## measure and draw, or the centering describes a different font).
  let w = textWidth(s, size, family, weight)
  d.textFace(cx - w * 0.5'f32, baseY - 1.0833'f32 * size, size, argb, family, s,
             weight)

proc strokeRect*(d: Draw; x, y, w, h, radius: float32; argb: uint32;
                 width: float32): Draw =
  ## Stroked rounded rectangle (the `B` verb `runCmds` already renders;
  ## `rect` only FILLS, which forced dna's double-fill `rrect` workaround).
  result = d
  result.add "B " & f(x) & " " & f(y) & " " & f(w) & " " & f(h) & " " &
             f(radius) & " " & $argb & " " & f(width)

proc strokeCircle*(d: Draw; cx, cy, r: float32; argb: uint32;
                   width: float32): Draw =
  ## Stroked circle as a `B` verb (gallery hand-rolled this via `rectStroke`).
  result = d.strokeRect(cx - r, cy - r, r * 2.0'f32, r * 2.0'f32, r, argb, width)


================================================================
FILE: sdk/core/signals.nim
================================================================
## Yantra SDK Core — fine-grained reactive signals, effects, owners and batched writes (L1, L7).
## Pure Nim — compiles both natively and to wasm32 (--cpu:wasm32 --os:standalone --mm:arc).
##
## Three ideas, as in docs/spec/core.md §5:
##   * a `Signal` holds a value and knows which effects read it;
##   * an `Effect` re-runs when a signal it read in its latest run changes. Before
##     every run it forgets its old dependencies and disposes what it created, so it
##     only ever depends on what it actually read last time [S-RX-5];
##   * an `Owner` is a scope. Effects, child scopes and clean-ups belong to the
##     scope that was current when they were created. Disposing a scope disposes all
##     of it, children first, in reverse creation order [S-RX-9]. A list item or an
##     `if` branch is one scope, which is how their nodes and effects go away.

import std/typetraits
import hotstate
export hotstate

type
  Owner* = ref object
    parent: Owner
    children: seq[Owner]
    effects: seq[Effect]
    cleanups: seq[proc()]
    disposed: bool

  SignalBase* = ref object of RootObj
    subscribers: seq[Effect]

  Effect* = ref object
    orderId: int
    fn: proc()
    cleanups: seq[proc()]
    deps: seq[SignalBase]
    scope: Owner          ## what the latest run created; disposed before the next run
    owner: Owner          ## the scope this effect belongs to
    disposed: bool
    queued: bool

  Signal*[T] = ref object of SignalBase
    value: T

var
  currentEffect: Effect = nil
  currentOwner: Owner = nil
  trackingEnabled: bool = true
  nextEffectOrderId: int = 0
  batchDepth: int = 0
  pendingEffects: seq[Effect] = @[]
  flushing: bool = false
  lastOverflowDetected: bool = false
  runsThisFlush: seq[(Effect, int)]

proc hasReactiveOverflow*(): bool =
  ## Returns true if the most recent effect flush hit the 100-run cycle guard.
  lastOverflowDetected

# --- owners ------------------------------------------------------------------

proc newOwner*(parent: Owner = currentOwner): Owner =
  ## A new scope under `parent` (by default, the current one).
  result = Owner(parent: parent)
  if parent != nil and not parent.disposed:
    parent.children.add(result)

proc getOwner*(): Owner = currentOwner

proc isDisposed*(o: Owner): bool = o == nil or o.disposed

proc dispose*(eff: Effect)

proc disposeOwner*(o: Owner) =
  ## Disposes a scope: child scopes, then effects, then clean-ups, each in reverse
  ## creation order. Idempotent.
  if o == nil or o.disposed: return
  o.disposed = true
  for i in countdown(o.children.high, 0): disposeOwner(o.children[i])
  o.children.setLen(0)
  for i in countdown(o.effects.high, 0): dispose(o.effects[i])
  o.effects.setLen(0)
  for i in countdown(o.cleanups.high, 0): o.cleanups[i]()
  o.cleanups.setLen(0)
  if o.parent != nil and not o.parent.disposed:
    let idx = o.parent.children.find(o)
    if idx >= 0: o.parent.children.delete(idx)

proc runWithOwner*(o: Owner; fn: proc()) =
  ## Runs `fn` with `o` as the current scope, outside any effect and untracked:
  ## what `fn` creates belongs to `o`, and what it reads subscribes nothing.
  let prevOwner = currentOwner
  let prevEff = currentEffect
  let prevTrack = trackingEnabled
  currentOwner = o
  currentEffect = nil
  trackingEnabled = false
  try:
    fn()
  finally:
    currentOwner = prevOwner
    currentEffect = prevEff
    trackingEnabled = prevTrack

# --- effects -----------------------------------------------------------------

proc unsubscribeAll(eff: Effect) =
  for dep in eff.deps:
    let i = dep.subscribers.find(eff)
    if i >= 0: dep.subscribers.delete(i)
  eff.deps.setLen(0)

proc runEffectInternal(eff: Effect) =
  if eff == nil or eff.disposed:
    return
  for i in countdown(eff.cleanups.high, 0):
    eff.cleanups[i]()
  eff.cleanups.setLen(0)
  disposeOwner(eff.scope)
  unsubscribeAll(eff)
  eff.scope = Owner(parent: nil)
  let prevEff = currentEffect
  let prevTrack = trackingEnabled
  let prevOwner = currentOwner
  currentEffect = eff
  trackingEnabled = true
  currentOwner = eff.scope
  try:
    eff.fn()
  finally:
    currentEffect = prevEff
    trackingEnabled = prevTrack
    currentOwner = prevOwner

proc noteRun(eff: Effect): bool =
  ## Counts a run of `eff` in this flush; false once it has run more than 100
  ## times — an effect that keeps re-triggering itself is a cycle [S-RX-7].
  for i in 0 ..< runsThisFlush.len:
    if runsThisFlush[i][0] == eff:
      inc runsThisFlush[i][1]
      return runsThisFlush[i][1] <= 100
  runsThisFlush.add((eff, 1))
  true

proc flush() =
  if flushing or batchDepth > 0:
    return
  flushing = true
  lastOverflowDetected = false
  runsThisFlush.setLen(0)
  while pendingEffects.len > 0:
    # Earliest-created first: parents before children, stable order [S-RX-4, S-RX-8].
    var best = 0
    for i in 1 ..< pendingEffects.len:
      if pendingEffects[i].orderId < pendingEffects[best].orderId: best = i
    let eff = pendingEffects[best]
    pendingEffects.delete(best)
    eff.queued = false
    if eff.disposed: continue
    if not noteRun(eff):
      for e in pendingEffects: e.queued = false
      pendingEffects.setLen(0)
      runsThisFlush.setLen(0)
      flushing = false
      lastOverflowDetected = true
      when not defined(standalone):
        raise newException(ValueError, "ReactiveOverflow: effect cycle exceeded 100 iterations")
      else:
        return
    runEffectInternal(eff)
  flushing = false
  runsThisFlush.setLen(0)

proc schedule(subs: seq[Effect]) =
  for eff in subs:
    if eff != nil and not eff.disposed and not eff.queued:
      eff.queued = true
      pendingEffects.add(eff)
  if batchDepth == 0 and not flushing:
    flush()

# --- signals -------------------------------------------------------------------

proc newState*[T](initial: T): Signal[T] =
  Signal[T](value: initial, subscribers: newSeqOfCap[Effect](4))

proc keepAcrossReloads*[T](s: Signal[T]; file: string; line: int) =
  ## Registers `s` with hot reload (sdk/core/hotstate.nim), if its type can be saved.
  ## Release builds leave hot reload out entirely: it costs them nothing.
  when not defined(release) and compiles((var b = ""; packInto(b, s.value))):
    hotRegister(file, line, name(T),
      proc (): string = packInto(result, s.value),
      proc (data: string): bool =
        var x: T
        var p = 0
        if unpackFrom(data, p, x) and p == data.len:
          s.value = x
          true
        else: false)

template state*(initial: untyped): untyped =
  ## A signal holding `initial`. It keeps its value when the app is hot-reloaded.
  let s = newState(initial)
  when not defined(release):
    keepAcrossReloads(s, instantiationInfo(-1).filename, instantiationInfo(-1).line)
  s

proc track(s: SignalBase) =
  if trackingEnabled and currentEffect != nil and not currentEffect.disposed:
    if s.subscribers.find(currentEffect) < 0:
      s.subscribers.add(currentEffect)
      currentEffect.deps.add(s)

proc get*[T](s: Signal[T]): lent T =
  ## Reads and subscribes. Borrowed, not copied: reading a list of 100 000 items costs
  ## nothing (copy it with `var x = s.get` to change it).
  track(s)
  s.value

proc peek*[T](s: Signal[T]): lent T =
  ## Reads without subscribing (borrowed, like `get`).
  s.value

proc `$`*[T](s: Signal[T]): string =
  $(s.get())

converter val*[T](s: Signal[T]): T =
  ## Implicit unwrap of a Signal[T] to its inner value T while tracking subscriptions [S-RX-1].
  s.get()

proc set*[T](s: Signal[T]; v: T) =
  if s.value == v:
    return
  s.value = v
  schedule(s.subscribers)

proc update*[T](s: Signal[T]; fn: proc(v: var T)) =
  ## Mutates in place and always notifies [S-RX-3].
  fn(s.value)
  schedule(s.subscribers)

proc `+=`*[T](s: Signal[T]; delta: T) =
  s.set(s.value + delta)

proc `-=`*[T](s: Signal[T]; delta: T) =
  s.set(s.value - delta)

proc createEffect*(fn: proc()): Effect =
  ## Runs `fn` now and again whenever a signal it read changes. Belongs to the
  ## current scope, so disposing that scope stops it.
  inc nextEffectOrderId
  result = Effect(orderId: nextEffectOrderId, fn: fn, owner: currentOwner)
  if currentOwner != nil and not currentOwner.disposed:
    currentOwner.effects.add(result)
  if flushing:
    runEffectInternal(result)
  else:
    inc batchDepth
    try:
      runEffectInternal(result)
    finally:
      dec batchDepth
    if batchDepth == 0: flush()

proc onCleanup*(fn: proc()) =
  ## Inside an effect: runs before its next run and when it is disposed.
  ## Otherwise: runs when the current scope is disposed.
  if currentEffect != nil and not currentEffect.disposed:
    currentEffect.cleanups.add(fn)
  elif currentOwner != nil and not currentOwner.disposed:
    currentOwner.cleanups.add(fn)

proc dispose*(eff: Effect) =
  if eff == nil or eff.disposed:
    return
  eff.disposed = true
  for i in countdown(eff.cleanups.high, 0):
    eff.cleanups[i]()
  eff.cleanups.setLen(0)
  disposeOwner(eff.scope)
  unsubscribeAll(eff)
  let o = eff.owner
  if o != nil and not o.disposed:
    let i = o.effects.find(eff)
    if i >= 0: o.effects.delete(i)

proc batch*(fn: proc()) =
  inc batchDepth
  try:
    fn()
  finally:
    dec batchDepth
  if batchDepth == 0 and not flushing:
    flush()

proc untrack*[T](fn: proc(): T): T =
  let prevTrack = trackingEnabled
  trackingEnabled = false
  try:
    result = fn()
  finally:
    trackingEnabled = prevTrack

proc derived*[T](fn: proc(): T): Signal[T] =
  ## A value computed from other signals and kept up to date [S-RX-5].
  let s = Signal[T](subscribers: newSeqOfCap[Effect](4))
  discard createEffect(proc() = s.set(fn()))
  s

template derive*(body: untyped): untyped =
  ## `let total = derive: items.get.len * price.get` — `derived` without the proc (S11).
  derived(proc (): auto = body)


================================================================
FILE: sdk/panicoverride.nim
================================================================
## Required by Nim's --os:standalone: the freestanding target has no libc, so the
## compiler demands you supply these two hooks yourself. This is the empirical
## answer to "is Nim->wasm a supported first-class target?" - it compiles, but YOU
## are the runtime. That is precisely the shape an embedded app sandbox needs, and
## precisely why it is not a "free" target.

proc panic(msg: string) {.nimcall.} =
  ## Host-side trap. In a real Yantra app module this would call the host's
  ## `yantra_panic` import so the engine can attribute the fault to this module.
  discard msg

proc rawoutput(msg: string) {.nimcall.} =
  ## stderr equivalent. No filesystem, no console: drop it, or forward to the host.
  discard msg

FILE: apps/dna/052-dna-helix.txt
================================================================
TITLE: Helix — The Double Helix Explained
FILE: 052-dna-helix.html

=== PROMPT (exact prompt used) ===
Build "Helix — The Double Helix Explained", an educational, beautiful interactive visualization. Centre: a rotating B-DNA double helix drawn on canvas with 3D projection — two sugar-phosphate backbones as glossy ribbons (#2ec4b6 and #e71d36 with depth shading) and base pairs as rungs coloured by nucleotide (A #ff9f1c, T #ffbf69, G #6a4c93, C #8ac926), with correct pairing (A–T, G–C), ~10.5 base pairs per turn, and depth fading for the back strands. A clean biotech palette on off-white (#f7f9fb) with a dark-mode toggle. A side panel with annotations and leader lines (major/minor groove; hydrogen bonds: 2 for A–T, 3 for G–C; 5'→3' direction), a sequence input where typing A/T/G/C rebuilds the helix live (auto-generating the complementary strand), and an "Unzip" button that animates replication — the strands separate and new complementary nucleotides float in. Interaction: drag to rotate, scroll to zoom, hover a base pair to highlight and label it. Typography: a modern sans with a monospace sequence display.

SHARED REQUIREMENTS (apply to every page in this 100-page collection):
1. One standalone HTML5 file. All CSS inline in <style>, all JavaScript inline in <script>. No external resources of any kind: no CDN, no libraries, no web fonts, no external images/SVG/audio files, no network requests. All visuals come from CSS, inline SVG, Canvas and procedural code.
2. Valid HTML5: <!DOCTYPE html>, <html lang="en">, <meta charset="utf-8">, a viewport meta tag, a meaningful <title> and meta description, well-nested semantic markup, unique ids, labels/aria attributes for controls, <button type="button"> for actions.
3. Typography from local system font stacks only, always ending in a generic family; display lettering may be drawn with SVG or Canvas.
4. Responsive from 360px phones to large desktops with no horizontal page scroll; canvases match their container and devicePixelRatio (capped at 2) and re-layout on resize.
5. Works offline by opening the file directly (file://); no console errors; storage access guarded with try/catch.
6. Smooth motion via requestAnimationFrame and transform/opacity; animation pauses when the tab is hidden where it matters; honours prefers-reduced-motion with a calmer fallback.
7. Pointer events so mouse and touch both work; visible :focus-visible styles; keyboard support where it makes sense.
8. Any sound is synthesized with the Web Audio API, starts only after a user gesture, and has a visible mute/toggle.
9. Premium finish: a deliberate palette and spacing, polished micro-interactions, a beautiful first frame before any interaction, and original copy (no lorem ipsum). Factual content must be accurate; fictional brands, people and data are clearly fictional.
10. Quality bar: visually impressive, bookmark-worthy, showcase-grade, premium, full of delightful details, and clearly distinct from the other 99 pages in concept, layout, palette, typography and motion.


=== DESCRIPTION ===
Helix is a clean, editorial biotech explainer. On the left, a large canvas on an off-white dotted-grid "lab bench" (#f7f9fb) shows an idealised B-DNA double helix, rendered in true 3D with perspective. It is right-handed, has 10.5 base pairs per turn and a 0.34 nm rise, and its two backbones are offset about 135° around the axis, which produces a visibly wide major groove and a narrow minor groove. The helix slowly rotates on a slightly diagonal axis, beside a bold "The Double Helix" headline and a one-turn scale bracket. On the right, a white annotation panel lists five numbered features (5′→3′ direction, sugar–phosphate backbone, major groove, minor groove, hydrogen bonds). Live leader lines run from each note to numbered pins that track the matching feature as the molecule turns. Below the notes are the key measurements, a sequence builder with a colour-coded complementary strand, and a legend. An "Unzip & replicate" sequence animates a simplified semi-conservative replication. A dark mode moves the whole piece to deep navy.

=== MAJOR VISUAL TECHNIQUES ===
- Hand-written 3D pipeline on canvas 2D: model coordinates in ångström, spin about the helix axis, tilt, perspective divide, then an in-plane roll (diagonal on wide screens, near-vertical on phones)
- Correct B-DNA geometry: right-handed twist of 360°/10.5 per bp, 3.4 Å rise, 10 Å radius, 135° backbone offset giving roughly 13 Å and 22 Å grooves; antiparallel strands labelled 5′/3′ at their ends
- Glossy backbone ribbons (#2ec4b6, #e71d36) built from per-segment quads, lit by a Lambert and specular term from the ribbon normal, with phosphate beads and white 5′→3′ chevrons
- Base-pair rungs split at a purine/pyrimidine-weighted point and coloured by nucleotide (A #ff9f1c, T #ffbf69, G #6a4c93, C #8ac926), with specular highlight lines and dashed hydrogen bonds (two for A=T, three for G≡C)
- Depth handled by pooled draw items sorted back to front (painter's algorithm), plus depth fading of back elements toward the page colour (works in both themes)
- Live leader lines: an SVG overlay connects panel anchors to the front-most matching feature each frame (a groove centre, a backbone point, an A–T and a G–C pair); canvas pins carry the numbers
- Replication: a moving fork separates the strands onto two offset axes, free nucleotides fly in along eased wobbling paths, then new lighter-tinted backbones join up into two daughter helices
- Pop-in growth for bases that change when the sequence is edited; a dotted-grid CSS background; a theme-aware palette

=== INTERACTION MODEL ===
- Drag to rotate: movement across the axis spins the helix and movement along it tilts it; spin keeps its inertia and eases back into the auto-rotation. Scroll wheel or pinch to zoom; buttons for pause/play, zoom and reset; keyboard (arrows rotate and tilt, +/− zoom, Space pauses)
- Hover a rung (or tap on touch) to highlight that base pair: a tooltip names both bases, purine or pyrimidine, and the number of hydrogen bonds, and the matching column lights up in the sequence display. Hovering letters in the sequence display highlights the pair in 3D
- Hover an annotation to emphasise its leader line and pin
- Sequence input accepts A/T/G/C only (other characters are stripped with a gentle warning), 2 to 40 bases; the helix rebuilds live with the complementary strand, and GC %, hydrogen-bond count, turns, length and a basic Tm estimate update. A Random button generates a new sequence
- "Unzip & replicate" runs the fork and synthesis with stage captions, then becomes "Rejoin strands" to reverse it
- The dark-mode toggle is saved in localStorage (guarded with try/catch); rendering pauses when the tab is hidden; reduced motion slows rotation and speeds up the replication steps

FILE: apps/dna/052-dna-helix.html
================================================================

    <h2>Anatomy</h2>
    <ol class="anns" id="anns">
      <li class="ann" data-k="dir"><span class="pin">1</span><div><h3>5′ → 3′ direction</h3><p>The strands are antiparallel. The teal strand runs 5′→3′ along the axis; its red partner runs 5′→3′ the opposite way. The small chevrons show each strand's direction.</p></div></li>
      <li class="ann" data-k="bb"><span class="pin">2</span><div><h3>Sugar–phosphate backbone</h3><p>Each strand's outer rail: deoxyribose sugars and phosphate groups joined by phosphodiester bonds. The phosphates give DNA its negative charge.</p></div></li>
      <li class="ann" data-k="maj"><span class="pin">3</span><div><h3>Major groove</h3><p>The wide gap, about 2.2 nm across. The edges of the bases are most exposed here, so many DNA-binding proteins read the sequence through it.</p></div></li>
      <li class="ann" data-k="min"><span class="pin">4</span><div><h3>Minor groove</h3><p>The narrow gap, about 1.2 nm across. The grooves differ because the two backbones are not directly opposite each other around the axis.</p></div></li>
      <li class="ann" data-k="hb"><span class="pin">5</span><div><h3>Hydrogen bonds <span class="bond">A=T · 2</span><span class="bond">G≡C · 3</span></h3><p>Adenine pairs with thymine through two hydrogen bonds, and guanine with cytosine through three. A purine always faces a pyrimidine, so every rung is the same width.</p></div></li>
    </ol>

        <button type="button" class="btn" id="randBtn" aria-label="Random sequence"><svg viewBox="0 0 20 20" aria-hidden="true"><rect x="3" y="3" width="14" height="14" rx="3"/><circle cx="7" cy="7" r=".9" fill="currentColor"/><circle cx="13" cy="13" r=".9" fill="currentColor"/><circle cx="13" cy="7" r=".9" fill="currentColor"/><circle cx="7" cy="13" r=".9" fill="currentColor"/></svg>Random</button>
      </div>
      <p class="msg" id="seqMsg">Type A, T, G or C (2 to 40 bases). The partner strand is generated for you.</p>
      <div class="seq" aria-label="Sequence with its complementary strand">
        <div class="srow"><span class="end">5′</span><span class="bases" id="rowA"></span><span class="end r">3′</span></div>
        <div class="srow"><span class="end"></span><span class="bases hb" id="rowH" aria-hidden="true"></span><span class="end"></span></div>
        <div class="srow"><span class="end">3′</span><span class="bases" id="rowB"></span><span class="end r">5′</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="stats" id="stats"></div>
      <button type="button" class="btn primary" id="unzipBtn"><svg viewBox="0 0 20 20" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M10 2v5M10 7 5 18M10 7l5 11M7.3 12h5.4"/></svg><span id="unzipLbl">Unzip &amp; replicate</span></button>
    </div>

    <h2>Legend</h2>
    <div class="legend">
      <div class="lg"><i style="background:#ff9f1c"></i><span>Adenine (A)<small>purine · pairs with T</small></span></div>
      <div class="lg"><i style="background:#ffbf69"></i><span>Thymine (T)<small>pyrimidine · pairs with A</small></span></div>
      <div class="lg"><i style="background:#6a4c93"></i><span>Guanine (G)<small>purine · pairs with C</small></span></div>
      <div class="lg"><i style="background:#8ac926"></i><span>Cytosine (C)<small>pyrimidine · pairs with G</small></span></div>
    </div>
    <div class="strands"><span><i style="background:#2ec4b6"></i>Top strand</span><span><i style="background:#e71d36"></i>Partner strand</span><span><i style="background:linear-gradient(90deg,#8ae2d8,#f48c9a)"></i>Newly built strands</span></div>
    <p class="note">The geometry follows idealised B-DNA: 10.5 bp per turn, 0.34 nm rise, about 2 nm across, with the two backbones offset by roughly 135° around the axis. Replication here is simplified: helicase, primase and polymerase are left out, and both new strands are drawn growing in the same direction.</p>
  </aside>
</div>
<svg id="leaders" aria-hidden="true"></svg>

<script>
(() => {
'use strict';
const $ = id => document.getElementById(id);
const mq = window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)');
let reduced = mq.matches;
if (mq.addEventListener) mq.addEventListener('change', e => { reduced = e.matches; });

/* ---------- theme ---------- */
const root = document.documentElement, themeBtn = $('themeBtn');
const MONO = getComputedStyle(root).getPropertyValue('--mono').trim() || 'monospace';
const SANS = getComputedStyle(root).getPropertyValue('--sans').trim() || 'sans-serif';
let dark = false;
try { dark = localStorage.getItem('helix-theme') === 'dark'; } catch (e) {}
function applyTheme(){
  root.dataset.theme = dark ? 'dark' : 'light';
  themeBtn.setAttribute('aria-pressed', dark ? 'true' : 'false');
  document.querySelector('meta[name="theme-color"]').setAttribute('content', dark ? '#0b1220' : '#f7f9fb');
}
applyTheme();
themeBtn.addEventListener('click', () => { dark = !dark; applyTheme(); try { localStorage.setItem('helix-theme', dark ? 'dark' : 'light'); } catch (e) {} });

/* =====================================================================
   Model constants (Ångström)
   ===================================================================== */
const MAXN = 40, SUB = 4;
const TWIST = 2 * Math.PI / 10.5, RISE = 3.4, RAD = 10, DELTA = 135 * Math.PI / 180, FOCAL = 95, HW = 1.55, RB = 8.4;
const PAIR = { A: 'T', T: 'A', G: 'C', C: 'G' };
const PUR = { A: true, G: true, T: false, C: false };
const NAME = { A: 'Adenine', T: 'Thymine', G: 'Guanine', C: 'Cytosine' };
const BASE = { A: [255, 159, 28], T: [255, 191, 105], G: [106, 76, 147], C: [138, 201, 38] };
const STR = [[46, 196, 182], [231, 29, 54], [244, 140, 154], [138, 226, 216]];

let seq = [], comp = [], n = 0;
const grow = new Float32Array(MAXN).fill(1);
const sep = new Float32Array(MAXN), arrA = new Float32Array(MAXN), arrB = new Float32Array(MAXN);
const spawnA = new Float32Array(MAXN * 3), spawnB = new Float32Array(MAXN * 3), delA = new Float32Array(MAXN), delB = new Float32Array(MAXN);
let mode = 'duplex', mt = 0;

/* =====================================================================
   Canvas + projection
   ===================================================================== */
const viz = $('viz'), cv = $('helix'), ctx = cv.getContext('2d');
let W = 0, H = 0, DPR = 1, landscape = true, cx = 0, cy = 0, k = 10, roll = 0, cR = 1, sR = 0;
const view = { spin: 0.6, spinV: 0, tilt: 0.16, tiltT: 0.16, zoom: 1, zoomT: 1, auto: true };
let cT = 1, sT = 0;
let PX = 0, PY = 0, PD = 0, PS = 1;
function proj(u, v, w){
  const u2 = u * cT - w * sT, w2 = u * sT + w * cT, s = FOCAL / (FOCAL - w2);
  const x = u2 * s * k, y = v * s * k;
  PX = cx + x * cR - y * sR; PY = cy + x * sR + y * cR; PD = w2; PS = s;
}
function layout(){
  const r = viz.getBoundingClientRect();
  DPR = Math.min(2, window.devicePixelRatio || 1); W = Math.max(1, r.width); H = Math.max(1, r.height);
  cv.width = Math.round(W * DPR); cv.height = Math.round(H * DPR);
  ctx.setTransform(DPR, 0, 0, DPR, 0, 0);
  landscape = W > H * 1.05;
  measureAnchors();
}
function frameGeometry(){
  roll = landscape ? -0.24 : -Math.PI / 2 + 0.38; cR = Math.cos(roll); sR = Math.sin(roll);
  cT = Math.cos(view.tilt); sT = Math.sin(view.tilt);
  const L = landscape ? W * 0.8 : Math.min(W / Math.abs(cR), (H - 90) / Math.abs(sR)) * 0.8;
  const risePx = Math.min(landscape ? 46 : W / 14, L / Math.max(n, 8));
  k = risePx / RISE * view.zoom;
  cx = landscape ? W * 0.47 : W / 2; cy = landscape ? H * 0.56 : H * 0.55;
}

/* ---------- colour ---------- */
let BG = [247, 249, 251];
function bgNow(){ return dark ? [11, 18, 32] : [247, 249, 251]; }
function paint(rgb, lit, spec, depth, alpha){
  const fade = Math.pow(Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (RAD - depth) / (2 * RAD))), 1.25) * (dark ? .66 : .6);
  let r = rgb[0] * lit + 255 * spec, g = rgb[1] * lit + 255 * spec, b = rgb[2] * lit + 255 * spec;
  r = Math.min(255, r + (BG[0] - r) * fade); g = Math.min(255, g + (BG[1] - g) * fade); b = Math.min(255, b + (BG[2] - b) * fade);
  return 'rgba(' + (r | 0) + ',' + (g | 0) + ',' + (b | 0) + ',' + alpha.toFixed(3) + ')';
}
const LU = -.28, LV = -.5, LW = .82;
function light(th){
  const nv = Math.cos(th), nw0 = -Math.sin(th);
  const nu = -nw0 * sT, nw = nw0 * cT;
  const d = nu * LU + nv * LV + nw * LW;
  const lit = .42 + .58 * Math.max(0, d) + (nw < 0 ? -.08 : 0);
  const spec = Math.pow(Math.max(0, d), 22) * .65;
  return [lit, spec];
}

/* ---------- item pool (painter's algorithm) ---------- */
const pool = [], list = [];
let pc = 0;
function item(t, d){
  let o = pool[pc];
  if (!o) { o = { t: 0, d: 0, x0: 0, y0: 0, x1: 0, y1: 0, x2: 0, y2: 0, x3: 0, y3: 0, w: 0, c: '', c2: '', n: 0, a: 1, hl: 0 }; pool[pc] = o; }
  pc++; o.t = t; o.d = d; o.hl = 0; o.a = 1; return o;
}

/* strand sample buffers: xL,yL,xR,yR,depth,x,y per sample */
const SB = [0, 1, 2, 3].map(() => new Float32Array(((MAXN - 1) * SUB + 1) * 7));
const easeOut = x => 1 - Math.pow(1 - x, 3);
const smooth = x => x <= 0 ? 0 : x >= 1 ? 1 : x * x * (3 - 2 * x);
const DSEP = RAD * 1.32;
function axisOff(which, s){ return (which === 0 || which === 2 ? 1 : -1) * DSEP * smooth(s); }
function angleOf(which){ return which === 1 || which === 2 ? DELTA : 0; }
function sampleStrand(si){
  const buf = SB[si], m = (n - 1) * SUB + 1;
  for (let s = 0; s < m; s++) {
    const t = s / SUB, i0 = Math.floor(t), i1 = Math.min(n - 1, i0 + 1), f = t - i0;
    const sp = sep[i0] + (sep[i1] - sep[i0]) * f;
    const off = axisOff(si, sp), th = TWIST * t + view.spin + angleOf(si), u = (t - (n - 1) / 2) * RISE;
    const v = RAD * Math.cos(th) + off, w = -RAD * Math.sin(th);
    const o = s * 7;
    proj(u - HW, v, w); buf[o] = PX; buf[o + 1] = PY; const d0 = PD;
    proj(u + HW, v, w); buf[o + 2] = PX; buf[o + 3] = PY;
    proj(u, v, w); buf[o + 4] = (d0 + PD) / 2; buf[o + 5] = PX; buf[o + 6] = PY;
  }
}
function pushRibbon(si, alphaAt){
  const buf = SB[si], m = (n - 1) * SUB + 1, rgb = STR[si];
  for (let s = 0; s < m - 1; s++) {
    const a = alphaAt ? alphaAt(s) : 1;
    if (a <= 0.01) continue;
    const o = s * 7, p = o + 7, t = (s + .5) / SUB, th = TWIST * t + view.spin + angleOf(si);
    const L = light(th), d = (buf[o + 4] + buf[p + 4]) / 2;
    const it = item(0, d);
    it.x0 = buf[o]; it.y0 = buf[o + 1]; it.x1 = buf[o + 2]; it.y1 = buf[o + 3]; it.x2 = buf[p + 2]; it.y2 = buf[p + 3]; it.x3 = buf[p]; it.y3 = buf[p + 1];
    it.c = paint(rgb, L[0], L[1], d, a);
    if (s % SUB === 0 && s > 0 && s < m - SUB) { const bd = item(3, buf[o + 4] + .05); bd.x0 = buf[o + 5]; bd.y0 = buf[o + 6]; bd.w = 1.05 * k * FOCAL / (FOCAL - buf[o + 4]); bd.c = paint([255, 255, 255], .95, 0, buf[o + 4], a * .55); }
  }
}

/* one base half: backbone attachment (angle thX) to the split towards partner (angle thP) around an axis */
const HALF = { x0: 0, y0: 0, x1: 0, y1: 0, d: 0, s: 1, tx: 0, ty: 0, mx: 0, my: 0 };
function baseHalf(i, thX, thP, off, purine, g, du, dv, dw){
  const u = (i - (n - 1) / 2) * RISE + du;
  const xv = RB * Math.cos(thX) + off + dv, xw = -RB * Math.sin(thX) + dw;
  const pv = RB * Math.cos(thP) + off + dv, pw = -RB * Math.sin(thP) + dw;
  const f = (purine ? .545 : .455) - .032;
  const ev = xv + (pv - xv) * f * g, ew = xw + (pw - xw) * f * g;
  proj(u, xv, xw); HALF.x0 = PX; HALF.y0 = PY; const d0 = PD;
  proj(u, ev, ew); HALF.x1 = PX; HALF.y1 = PY; HALF.d = (d0 + PD) / 2; HALF.s = PS;
  const mf = purine ? .545 : .455;
  proj(u, xv + (pv - xv) * mf, xw + (pw - xw) * mf); HALF.mx = PX; HALF.my = PY;
}
function pushHalf(ch, alpha, hl){
  const it = item(1, HALF.d);
  it.x0 = HALF.x0; it.y0 = HALF.y0; it.x1 = HALF.x1; it.y1 = HALF.y1;
  it.w = 1.25 * k * HALF.s;
  it.c = paint(BASE[ch], 1, 0, HALF.d, alpha);
